景気の底堅さとインフレ圧力の根強さを確認。貿易
赤字はAI関連を含む設備投資需要などを背景に拡
大。非製造業PMIは拡大圏で推移する一方、仕入
価格指数の上昇は企業のコスト負担増を示唆

ユーロ圏総合PMIは節目の50超を維持。小売売上
高も小幅増加。ドイツの鉱工業生産は持ち直したが、
製造業受注は大型案件の反動で減少。景気は緩
やかな回復を維持するも、持続力には不透明感
欧
州
ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き
注目点② 日銀さくらレポートは企業活動の底堅さを再確認注目点①米欧で金利上昇。異なる背景と金融政策への含意

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

（注）ブレーク・イーブン・インフレ率は、名目国債利回りと物価連動国債（TIPS）実質利回りの
差で、市場が織り込む期待インフレ率の目安。実質予想短期金利はNY連銀が算出する

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5
米国10年債利回りの構成要素 経済指標のサプライズに対する実質予想短期金利の感応度

の1カ月前比変化幅を説明変数として、直近3カ月のデータを用いてローリング推

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
企業コメント概要
0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

げに伴う販売数量の減少も
個人消費

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に
 今次局面では、現状、主要拠点への直接被害は確認されておらず、一部工場の生産停止にとどまっている模様
―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

（注） 旅行収支要因は、非居住者による国内消費（インバウンド消費）を控除し、居住者に
よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦 Fed 決策與財報
2026 年 9 月 15 日
一、 9/14 美股下挫、美債殖利率曲線熊市趨平，且油價大漲
1. 股票指數：
⚫ 道瓊工業指數下跌 0.29%，收 52,421.20 點；
⚫ S&P 500 指數下挫 0.48%，收 7,619.98 點；
⚫ 那斯達克綜合指數下跌 0.56%，收 26,186.41 點；
⚫ 費城半導體指數下跌 5.86%，收 11,131.28 點。
2. 當日重要個股表現：
⚫ 在科技類股方面，輝達下跌約 3.4%、美光下跌 5.3%、邁威爾下跌 7.3%；

⚫ 通訊類股方面，Netflix 上漲 3.8%、Take-Two 上漲 3.5%、Fox 上漲 4.6%；
⚫ 軟體類股方面 ，ServiceNow、Adobe、Workday 等軟體股則上漲約 4% 至
7.4%。
3. 美國債市漲跌不一、殖利率曲線走勢熊市趨平。2 年期美國公債殖利率上升 3.24 個基
點至 4.66%，10 年期殖利率上升 2.06 個基點至 4.99%，10 年期殖利率一度升破 5%，
而 30 年期殖利率則下跌約 0.76 個基點至 5.34%。短率上升，反映市場對未來美國聯
準會升息的預期。
4. 油價因中東衝突與供給風險再度升高而上漲 ，WTI 原油上漲 1.81%，收在每桶 101.86
美元，布蘭特原油則上漲 1.56%，收在 106.24 美元。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. AI 開發放緩疑慮，壓抑硬體需求預期：Anthropic 執行長 Dario Amodei 於上週末
呼籲放慢前沿 AI 能力提升速度，並提出第三方安全評估與業界協調方案，使 AI 擴

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
張節奏成為新的不確定因素。我們研判，半導體位於 AI 建置支出的上游，股價對新
增算力投資的成長速度尤其敏感。即使既有訂單未立即改變，只要市場降低對未來數
季增速的期待，GPU、高頻寬記憶體及網通設備供應鏈的評價便可能先行調整；目前
仍須區分投資預期降溫與實際訂單下修。
2. 中東能源設施遇襲，油價推升通膨風險：沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施及中東船舶遇襲，引
發供應疑慮，布蘭特原油期貨 9 月 14 日結算價升至每桶 106.24 美元。油價持續偏
高可能透過運輸與生產成本以及民眾實質購買力影響企業獲利；若進一步傳導至通膨

預期，將延長貨幣政策維持緊縮的時間，對股市評價形成壓力。
3. 十年美債殖利率一度突破 5%，評價與融資承壓：美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中突破
5%，為 2023 年以來首見。殖利率上升提高未來獲利的折現率，對依賴長期成長預
期支撐評價的股票較不利。另一方面，資料中心若仰賴借款擴張，融資成本上升也會
提高投資報酬門檻，可能影響新增建置計畫，形成 AI 供應鏈面臨的另一項壓力。
4. 通膨月增回升 ， 升息前觀望氣氛加重 ：BLS 於 9 月 11 日公布 ，8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%
，高於 7 月的 0.1%，核心 CPI 月增率也由 0.2% 升至 0.3%。我們認為，短期物
價動能回升，加上能源價格偏高，提高聯準會維持緊縮政策的壓力。投資人除了評估
當次升息，也須重新衡量後續利率高點與維持時間，增加 FOMC 會議前高波動股票
的調整壓力。
5. 成長與評價雙重重估，放大半導體跌幅：本波股市回檔壓力同時來自獲利成長預期與
市場願給的評價倍數。AI 開發放緩疑慮影響未來營收想像，殖利率上行則壓低未來獲

利的現值。當股價已反映較高成長期待，只要新增訊息不如預期，即使當期營收仍強
勁，也可能出現明顯修正。後續應觀察獲利上修能否抵銷評價收縮，不能僅以跌幅大
就認定股價便宜。

不另行通知。
三、市場看法
1. 龍頭財報仍具成長，支持中期正向看法：輝達 8 月 26 日公布的 2027 會計年度第
二季資料中心營收達 890 億美元、年增 117%，並預估第三季總營收 1,080 億美元
、上下浮動 2%。博通 9 月 2 日公布的 2026 會計年度第三季 AI 半導體營收為
167 億美元、年增 221%，並預估第四季達 217 億美元。上述實績與公司展望，是
我們對 AI 核心供應鏈維持中期正向看法的依據；惟這些財測發布於本次事件之前，
仍須追蹤後續更新。
2. 放緩方案尚待落地，需求影響須分項評估：Anthropic 執行長 Amodei 明確表示，放

緩不代表停止模型訓練，但也提出研議訓練算力等限制，因此實際影響取決於後續執
行方式。既有模型的商業使用、推論與安全測試仍可能帶來算力需求，惟能否抵銷前
沿訓練投資的放緩，必須由使用量、訂單與收入驗證。此時適合保留成長觀點，同時
提高對支出變化的敏感度。
3. 科技股仍有輪動，軟體與半導體強弱分明：9/14 當日企業軟體與資安股逆勢上漲，顯
示科技股內部仍有輪動與受惠程度的差異。我們認為，若前沿 AI 開發放緩，部分既
有軟體業者面臨的競爭及產品替代壓力可能減輕；AI 安全疑慮升高，也可能強化資安
、權限控管及企業治理需求。相較之下，半導體更直接承受新增建置支出預期變化。
因此，9/14 當日費半重挫尚不足以推論整體科技需求同步轉弱；後續宜比較訂單能見
度、訂閱收入、獲利與現金流，單日相對強勢仍須由基本面延續性驗證。
4. 升息機率約九成，會後政策態度才是關鍵：依 Reuters 於美東 9 月 14 日收盤報導
引述的 CME FedWatch 資料，本次升息一碼的機率約 90%。若美東 9 月 16 日

FOMC 確實升息 0.25 個百分點，利率區間將升至 3.75% 至 4.00%，當次升息已
大致反映於市場預期。我們認為，會後聲明及主席記者會對未來利率政策的態度才是
關鍵：是否暗示還需升息、利率須維持高檔多久，以及是否擔心能源漲價向核心通膨

### Page 4

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
擴散，並須搭配點陣圖判讀。若後續緊縮訊號未超出預期、長債殖利率趨穩，有利評
價修復；若利率路徑較預期更高、更持久，科技股仍可能承壓。
5. 通膨改善仍不均衡，避免過早判定轉折：8 月核心 CPI 年增率為 2.4%，低於 7 月
的 2.5%，但其月增率由 0.2% 升至 0.3%，顯示年增放緩與短期動能回升並存。後
續須觀察能源漲價是否向服務價格與通膨預期擴散。若油價回穩、核心物價再度降溫
，利率壓力可望緩和；若能源衝擊持續，股市調整時間可能拉長。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦 Fed 決策與 10 月美股財報，科技股中期向上趨勢料不致有變：我們目前仍

維持 AI 核心供應鏈中期正向看法，較看好能將營收成長轉化為獲利與現金流的企業
，但宜密切觀察本週的 FOMC 會議結果與 10 月中將起跑的美股財報。若雲端業者維
持資本支出、晶片公司財測穩定，且 FOMC 此次會議未釋出超預期緊縮訊號，有利
市場信心修復；然而，若出現廣泛支出下修、訂單遞延，或油價與殖利率持續上行，
則應拉長觀察期並重新檢視通膨與利率對於股市動能前景的影響。
2. 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：目前美國的通膨數據仍具韌性，且就業市場
穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨
仍要求較高風險補償，30 年期殖利率與短天期利差仍維持高檔，顯示期限溢酬尚未收
斂，考量當前 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收，且其受長端財政
風險影響也相對較小，故現階段3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對
較佳。

一、 美國—消費回暖、房市降溫，Fed 政策轉向與川習會成市場焦點 
2026 年 8 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節性調整後為 7,739 億美元，較
上月增加 1.2%，優於 7 月修正後月減 0.5%的表現，年增率亦達 6.0%，顯示美國
消費支出動能於 8 月明顯回升。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增1.4%、年增
6.9%；進一步扣除汽車、零件及加油站後，核心零售銷售月增1.2%、年增 5.6%，
反映核心消費需求維持穩健成長。從主要業別表現觀察，非實體零售商銷售額月
增 2.6%、加油站月增 3.1%、電子及家電商店月增 1.6%，食品服務及飲酒場所亦
月增 1.2%，顯示線上消費、能源相關支出及服務性消費持續成長；另一方面，建
築材料與園藝用品經銷商月減 0.2%，對整體零售表現形成部分抵銷。整體而言，
8 月美國零售銷售由前月下滑轉為明顯成長，且扣除汽車及能源相關項目後仍維
持穩健增幅，顯示美國消費需求仍具支撐力道，對經濟活動形成正面挹注，惟後
續仍須持續關注物價變動、金融情勢及消費信心對零售支出的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國住宅市場持續呈現分化走勢，建築許可年化總數降至 139.4
萬戶，月減 2.7%，惟較去年同期增加 3.5%；其中單戶住宅建築許可 87.8 萬戶，
月減 1.8%，5 戶以上多戶住宅建築許可 46.7 萬戶，月減 3.1%，顯示住宅開發規
劃動能較前月略有降溫 。 另一方面 ， 新屋開工年化數降至127.5 萬戶 ， 月減2.6%、
年減 1.2%，其中單戶住宅開工回升至 91.8 萬戶，月增 7.6%、年增 5.2%，為近 5
個月最高水準 ； 惟5 戶以上多戶住宅開工降至 34.4 萬戶 ， 月減22.5%、 年減15.5%，
拖累整體開工表現。施工方面，在建住宅年化數為 127.1 萬戶，月增 0.3%，但較
去年同期減少 3.2%，顯示住宅建設規模仍呈收斂趨勢；完工年化數則降至 112.8
萬戶，月減 11.9%、年減 27.1%，反映住宅供給落成速度明顯放緩。整體而言，8
月美國住宅市場呈現單戶住宅需求相對具支撐、但多戶住宅及整體營建活動偏弱
的格局，顯示在高利率與需求前景仍具不確定性的環境下，建商投資態度仍趨審
慎，後續仍須關注利率走勢及房市需求變化對住宅市場的影響。

下：(一)儘管地緣政治風險推升經濟前景不確定性，美國經濟活動仍維持穩健擴

最新 SEP 顯示，2026 年實質 GDP 成長率預測中位數由 2.2%上修至 2.3%，失業
率由 4.3%下修至 4.1%；PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨率則分別上修至 3.7%及 3.4%，顯

6 月轉趨鷹派；(五)主席 Kevin Warsh 表示，美國經濟與勞動市場維持韌性，通膨
仍高於目標且地緣政治風險升溫 ， 為本次升息主因 。 未來聯準會不提供前瞻指引 ，
也不預設利率路徑，將依經濟數據與展望機動調整政策。 
在 9 月 24 日美國華府白宮舉行之 「川習會」 前 ， 美國財長貝森特(Scott Bessent)
與中國國務院副總理何立峰於紐約摩根大通總部舉行會談，美國貿易代表葛里爾
(Jamieson Greer) 亦共同參與。雙方將就人工智慧 (AI)、關稅、貿易休戰及關鍵礦
產供應等議題交換意見，並為川普與習近平會晤預作準備。討論重點包括將於11
月 10 日到期之美中貿易休戰安排、中國稀土磁材及關鍵礦產出口供應情況，以
及 AI 模型安全與治理機制等議題。此外，雙方亦將檢視今年 5 月北京會談後尚
待落實之事項，包括降低部分非戰略性商品關稅、中國增加美國農產品採購及波
音客機採購計畫等。市場普遍預期本次會談將以維持既有合作架構及避免貿易緊
張升級為主要目標，短期內出現重大突破的可能性仍相對有限，美中關稅、關鍵
礦產供應及 AI 治理議題預料將成為本次川習會的重要焦點。 
美國政府近期在美墨加協定(USMCA)檢討談判中，要求墨西哥針對人工智慧
(AI)伺服器、半導體及相關硬體產品建立更嚴格的原產地規範，擬限制來自中國
等非北美地區零組件占比，並可能參照汽車產業的區域含量標準，以防堵外國企
業透過墨西哥組裝後出口美國，規避美國對中國商品實施的關稅與貿易限制。市
場分析指出，在美國 AI 基礎建設需求帶動下，墨西哥已成為重要 AI 伺服器生產

美國總統川普已於 9 月 18 日簽署《2026 年 Lindsey O. Graham 制裁俄羅斯與
伊朗法案》，該法案此前分別以參議院 86 票對 11 票、眾議院 262 票對 159 票通
過。法案除擴大對俄羅斯政府官員、金融機構及協助能源運輸的「影子艦隊」制
裁外，亦授權總統對持續大量購買俄羅斯石油及天然氣的第三國商品課徵最高
100%關稅，其中中國、印度等俄羅斯能源主要買家可能受到影響。法案目的在於
削弱俄羅斯能源出口收入及其支撐烏俄戰爭的財政來源，並強化對伊朗的相關制
裁。 
二、歐元區—美歐矛盾與歐中貿易協商在即，增添工業復甦變數 
 歐元區 7 月工業生產較 6 月下滑 0.1%，為連續兩個月衰退，其中非耐久消費
財產出減少 1.6%，抵銷中間財、資本財、消費耐久財分別成長0.3%、0.5%、0.9%
的影響，主要是美伊衝突帶來的庫存儲備需求持續消退，抵銷德國軍備採購及消
暑用品消費增加的影響。以國別而言，德國工業生產下跌1.5%最為疲軟，且為該
國近一年最大跌幅，主要是部分汽車大廠決定提前進行歲修，故預期待歲修結束
後，8、9 月份可望出現汽車庫存回補的生產需求，此外，在德國軍備採購需求的
支持下 ， 德國7 月工業訂單仍大幅成長 2.5%， 相關產出可望在第三季末及第四季
陸續反映；法、義、西工業生產月增率則分別為-0.4%、0.7%、0.6%。 
 整體而言，美伊衝突爆發後，能源密集產業普遍增產，但該類產業從 6 月起
已有較明顯的趨緩跡象，其他主要產業則大致呈現橫向波動，顯示工業生產趨勢
仍屬溫和。惟近期歐盟提議讓加拿大成為歐盟準會員國，引發美國不滿，可能進
一步升高美歐貿易與外交摩擦；此外，歐盟與中國預定於 10 月 8 日針對貿易失
衡問題進行重要協商 ， 後續若涉及貿易措施調整 ， 亦可能影響歐元區製造業表現 。

產業訂單減少影響，惟電子產品及電氣機械訂單持續成長，反映人工智慧相

關產業及供應鏈企業仍積極擴充產能，支撐資本支出動能。 雖然非製造業訂
單則因資通訊及資訊服務業需求回落影響而表現較弱，但在人力短缺與數位
轉型需求支撐下，投資動能仍具韌性。綜合來看，企業獲利穩健及AI 投資熱
潮延續，預期日本企業資本支出仍將維持成長態勢。 
8 月出口年增 19.3%，半導體相關產品仍為主要成長動能，其中半導體等
電子零件出口大幅成長 52.3%。從地區來看，對美國、中國及歐盟出口分別
成長 24.9%、20.6%及 11.0%，半導體等電子零組件對中國出口金額，以及對
美國 、 歐盟的半導體製造設備出口均較去年同期倍增，顯示全球 AI 及高科技
產業投資需求持續支撐日本出口表現。 
8 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 1.9%，主要受到政府恢復電力與天然
氣補貼，以及米價回落抑制食品價格所致。從細項來看，石油產品價格上漲
帶動家庭消費品等核心商品價格續漲；服務類通膨除通訊費用趨穩外，其他
服務項目已開始出現漲價跡象。展望未來，能源成本上升及企業將 薪資成本
逐步轉嫁至售價，預料將推動通膨壓力由商品擴散至更多服務領域，使通膨
維持緩步升溫趨勢，並進一步支持央行持續推動貨幣政策正常化。 
日本央行於 9 月 17、18 日召開貨幣政策會議，宣布將政策利率調升 1 碼
至 1.25%。雖然政策聲明整體基調變化不大，但仍持續強調通膨上行風險，
並指出目前金融條件仍相對寬鬆。 值得關注的是 ， 本次決策出現兩張反對票 ，
顯示委員會內部對經濟與通膨前景看法分歧加劇，一派認為當前經濟與通膨
動能尚不足以支持升息，另一派則認為通膨升溫風險高於預期。在會後記者
會上，總裁植田和男指出，日本貨幣政策已進入新的階段，由過去較重視通
膨低於目標的風險，轉為兼顧通膨上、下行風險，並強調未來升息步調將視
經濟與物價發展而定，不會預先承諾特定路徑。此外，他也表示 主要國家央
行升息對日本匯率及金融環境具有重要影響，因此將持續納入政策考量。雖
然總裁未透露最終利率水準，但相關談話暗示目前 1.25%的政策利率可能尚
未達到中性水準，日本央行亦未排除未來進一步升息的可能性。整體而言，
日本央行對通膨前景看法趨於樂觀，政策立場仍偏向緊縮，預期若未來數季
通膨持續走揚，日本央行仍有望於 12 月再度升息。

四、中國—8 月內需持續疲乏，與歐美的關係將為近期關注重點 
在內需方面，1-8 月累計零售額年增率較 2025 年同期增加 1.1%，較 1-7 月累
計的 1.2%持續下降。其中 8 月零售額年增率放緩 0.2 個百分點至 0.4%，反映以
舊換新的補貼力道不如過去兩年水準，加上房市低迷，以及對景氣波動較為敏感
的消費耐久財需求進一步下滑 ，8 月家具 、 汽車 、 裝潢消費分別年減7.9%、18.5%、
11.8%。另外，1-8 月固定資產投資年減幅由 6.7%擴大至 7.2%，反映美伊衝突、
政府主導去化過剩產能、美國貿易戰等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎縮產
業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等。同期間房地產開發
投資額年減幅由 19.2%擴大至 19.9%、施工面積年減幅則擴大為 13.2%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動 ， 抵銷內需疲軟的影響 ，8 月工業生產年增率較 7
月回升 0.7 個百分點至 5.2%(1-8 月工業生產年增率持平 1-7 月水準為 5.3%)。依
產業別來看，8 月份高科技產品仍是主要成長動能，包含 IC(成長 20.6%)、工業
機器人(34.6%)、服務機器人(5%)、 新能源車(21.9%)等 ； 惟受到原油供應減少以及
能源價格上漲壓力影響，化纖、水泥、玻璃、鋼材等產量持續衰退，另因 3C 關
鍵物料漲價影響，手機產量衰退幅度高達 22.3%，太陽能電池則因取消出口退稅
政策跌幅高達 12.9%。展望未來，若9 月 24 日川習會有正向發展，美中關稅休戰
得以延長 ， 且歐中貿易摩擦未進一步惡化 ， 將有助於穩定中國出口與製造業景氣 ，
抵銷內需疲軟的經濟壓力。 
五、印度及東南亞—印度通膨持穩，AI 需求持續帶動新、馬出口表現 
印度:受美伊衝突延宕與不利的氣候條件影響，8 月批發物價指數(WPI)年增率
揚升至 9.92%，其中，燃料價格飆漲22.9%，食品價格漲幅達 5.7%，另受化學
品、菸草、塑橡膠、基本金屬漲幅均逾一成影響，製造業通膨加速上揚8.4%。
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 4.82%，為 2024 年 12 月以來最高，食品
飲料（5.95%）、交通運輸（4.6%）餐飲和住宿服務（8.38%）及個人照護/社
會安全 （15.17%） 價格亦大幅上漲。儘管通膨水準仍維持在印度央行目標區間
內，惟生產成本居高不下，恐進一步外溢到消費端，並衝擊內需表現。 
東南亞:(1)新加坡：8 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 46.2%，為 1988 年 10
月以來最強勁的成長速度 ， 反映強勁的人工智慧相關需求 ， 帶動電子產品出口

躍增 131.8%，磁碟媒體產品、個人電腦出口成長均逾 2 倍；非電子產品出口
由衰退轉為成長 12%，主要因非貨幣黃金、專用機械出口成長逾5 成所致。在
貿易夥伴中 ， 對美國 （91%） 、 韓國 （87.1%） 、 中國 （70.3%） 、 香港 （62.9%）、
台灣（58.5%）、馬來西亞（39.3%）和印尼（24.5%）出口均有顯著成長，但
對歐盟出口反下滑 1.7%。(2)馬來西亞：8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率小幅
上升至 1.9%，主要因食品、酒精、其他商品與服務價格上揚速度加快，核心
通膨率小幅下降至 1.7%，為2024 年 12 月以來的最低 ， 通膨仍維持在央行1.5%
至 2.5%的目標區間內。8 月出口額年增 45.5%至 MYR1,911 億元，其中電子&
電氣產品出口大增 66.5%，持續反映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，礦產品出口亦大幅
成長 43.3%，抵銷農產品出口暴跌 21.4%的影響，對美國出口大增 130.4%、新
加坡 58.5%居次。8 月進口額成長 41.1%至 MYR1,630 億元，受惠出口引申需
求 ， 中間產品 、 資本貨物進口分別成長50.5%、37.4%， 消費品進口下滑1.6%，
自中國和新加坡進口分別成長 61.3%、59.1%。進出口相抵，8 月貿易順差達
MYR281 億元、年增 77.1%。 
五、台灣—央行維持利率不變並適度鬆綁不動產信用管制措施 
台灣央行於 9 月 17 日召開理監事會議，決議維持政策利率(重貼現率)於 2%
不變，央行指出，雖然國際油價維持高檔、服務類通膨仍具黏性，但國內通膨仍
屬可控範圍，2026 年 1 至 8 月通膨年增 1.84%，預估 2027 年可回落至 2%以下；
同時考量中東地緣政治、美國經貿政策及 AI 產業發展等不確定因素，因此採取
審慎觀望態度，暫不升息。 
此外，央行認為先前實施之選擇性信用管制已逐漸見效，房市交易降溫、不
動產貸款集中度下降，因此適度調整相關措施，包括將自然人第二戶購屋貸款最
高成數由 6 成提高至 7 成，並刪除購地貸款須切結於一定期間內動工興建之規
定，改由金融機構依授信實務審慎評估辦理。整體而言，本次政策方向呈現利率
維持審慎偏緊基調、房市管制微幅鬆綁。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 澳洲 8 月就業人數勁增 3.95 萬人大幅優於預期，但勞動參與率上升至 67.1%，
推升失業率至 4.6%，呈現勞動力擴增但市場邊際降溫跡象。澳洲央行總裁布拉
克日前指出，失業率回升至5%有助於抑制頑固通膨。考量物價長期高於目標中
點，市場預期澳洲央行本週二將重啟緊縮，升息 1 碼至 4.60%，創 2011年底以
來新高。
 瑞士央行（SNB）上週四宣布連續第五次將關鍵政策利率維持在0%不變，符合
市場預期。儘管受油價推升使 8 月通膨微升至 0.8%，但仍處於目標區間，央行
預期中期通膨壓力溫和，現行極度寬鬆立場依然適當。決議公布後瑞郎兌美元跌
至 1.2077。相較全球主要央行相繼緊縮，瑞士央行維持超低利率，策略轉為視情
況調節匯市以穩定瑞郎。
 標普全球公布歐元區 9 月綜合 PMI 初值升至 53.1，創三年新高，製造與服務業
同步擴張，新訂單增速達四年多最快，印證經濟無懼美伊衝突引發的能源成本飆

升，展現超預期韌性。受能源價格傳導加速影響，通膨壓力再度升溫，儘管尚未
浮現薪資二輪效應，但強勁景氣數據促使市場大幅提高歐洲央行提前於10月例
會再度升息的押注。
 美國 9 月綜合 PMI 初值勁升至 58.4，創 62 個月新高；製造業與服務業 PMI 亦
逆勢走強至 57與 58.7，凸顯企業擴張強勁，引發市場對通膨持續性的擔憂。市
場焦點轉向 9 月 30日公布的美國 8 月 PCE、個人消費支出與 Q2 GDP終值，同
日日本亦將發布短觀調查與央行意見摘要。若終端消費延續強勁，將牽動美日央
行政策利率預期。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.655，低點在 31.866，以 31.790作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 2.8分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，台股創新高，外資連續五日買超台股並匯入，推升
台幣最高來到 31.655，不過逢低吸引進口商、政府基金等買盤進場，台幣主要在
31.700 附近震盪；臨近周末，美國 9 月綜合 PMI 由 8 月的 56 走升至 58.4，創下逾
5 年來的新高，加上美伊戰事持續，皆推升市場對後續升息的預期，美元指數升至
101 上方，台幣跳空開低，回貶至 31.800 上方，最低來到 31.866，不過隨後出口商
賣壓出籠，台幣跌勢放緩，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.790。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國經濟表現仍具韌性，9月綜合
PMI 由 8 月的 56 上升至 58.4，企業活動加速至逾 5 年來最強，數據公布後美國 10
年債券殖利率升破 5%，Fed Watch 顯示市場對於聯準會 10 月升息預期升高，有助
於美元指數維持偏強格局，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，台股在創下
新高後回落，而外資也結束連續五日買超的走勢，轉為賣超，短線上資金匯入不利
台幣表現，然而，台灣8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6億美元、年增 71.4%，創歷史新高，
AI 需求帶動出口持續強勁，出口商後續拋匯需求預計將提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，
預估本週台幣區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走貶。人民幣中間價在上周連續十天走升，並升破6.7500整數
關口，最高來到 6.7468，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美伊戰事持續，市場對於聯準
會升息預期提升，美元指數震盪走強，加上週間公布美國9月綜合 PMI由 8 月的 56
走升至 58.4，創下逾 5 年來的新高，美元指數突破 101 整數關卡，令境外人民幣承
壓，USD/CNH回貶至 6.7200上方，終場境外人民幣收在6.7227，週線貶值283點，
境內人民幣則收在 6.7125，週線貶值 141 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣高檔震盪。就國際市場來看，美中兩國完成新一輪談判，美方
表示雙方同意將原訂 11 月 10 日到期的貿易休戰延長至明年 1 月 10 日，並持續就
關稅、貿易及供應鏈等議題進行協商，降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險，對人民幣形
成一定支撐。不過，美國近期經濟數據仍具韌性，加以聯準會偏鷹立場推升美元，
預計將限制人民幣升值空間；就中國國內市場來看，近期人行持續偏強設定中間價，
帶動人民幣維持偏強走勢，惟美中利差再度擴大，預計將限制人民幣升值的空間；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，匯價反彈至 20日均線之上，顯示原先下
行動能暫歇，上方壓力先看向季線 6.7350附近，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣高檔震
盪。

美元兌日圓匯率走勢

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY先上後下。週初，美國多位聯準會官員鷹派談話，帶動美元指數
持續上行，推升 USD/JPY延續前一週的走勢來到 157上方；週間，受美國 PMI 強
於預期與美債殖利率全面上行帶動，市場重新提高對聯準會後續升息路徑的押注，
帶動 USD/JPY大漲突破 159的關卡；臨近週末，日本首相高市早苗表態日圓被低估
是個問題，使市場提高對政策訊號與潛在干預的敏感度，帶動USD/JPY回吐大部分
漲幅，終場收在 157.26，週線上漲 56點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本財務大臣片山皋月上週
五罕見透露，美國總統川普在美日元首峰會上對日圓過度疲弱表達關切，首相高市
早苗亦回應日圓遭低估確屬問題。儘管近期美元指數維持在 101 上方，但在日本政
府可能干預的陰影下，預計將限縮日圓貶值的空間。二、美國9 月綜合 PMI產出指
數創逾五年新高，疊加美伊戰事推升油價突破100美元與柴油大漲，通膨壓力劇增。
美聯儲理事巴爾表態可能需再升息兩次，芝加哥聯儲古爾斯比也示警能源衝擊具持
續性，推升市場押注 10 月升息機率升至六成，預計將支撐 USD/JPY 走勢。三、就
技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY連日上漲一度站回年線但隨即失守，目前依舊承
壓於季線及年線下方，不過各天期均線趨平，且下方尚有月線支撐，短線尚無明確
的方向。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，歐元延續前一週偏弱的走勢，加上聯準會官員鷹
派發言，導致歐元跌破 1.1450的重要關卡；週間，儘管油價自高檔回落，但市場解
讀為能源成本仍處高位、難以迅速回落，推升對美國實質利率與利差優勢的預期。
加上歐洲在能源依賴與成長動能較弱下，相對於美國更難承受長期高利率環境，歐
元最低來到 1.1358；臨近週末，歐元跌深反彈，終場收在1.1391，週線下跌 91點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學家連
恩示警，第二波能源價格飆升將推升通膨，預計至2027年中期始回落至目標水準。
德國央行月報指出，受中東衝突、低天然氣庫存及明年醫療改革影響，通膨將居高
不下；夏季雖因出口疲弱與乾旱致增長趨緩，但在製造業回暖與財政支出挹注下，
估年底經濟有望重啟復甦，預計將提供歐元一定的支撐。二、美聯儲官員接連釋放
強烈鷹派信號，聖路易聯儲總裁穆薩萊姆直言當前 3.75%-4.00%利率仍偏寬鬆，呼
籲盡早收緊；芝加哥聯儲總裁古爾斯比亦警告，AI投資繁榮引發需求過熱，疊加持
續性供應衝擊，可能迫使美聯儲加速且更積極地升息。目前Fedwatch顯示預期明年
4 月前可能再升息三次，將不利歐元表現。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
在各天期均線下方，且接連失守1.15及 1.14整數關卡，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.804% ~ 0.830%  
30天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.69%  
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95%  
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.84%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本周央行持續每日減發定存單，令市場資金充裕，帶動短
率靠向區間低位。 
週二央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.638%，略低於
市場預期，且較上次標售結果下跌 2.5個基本點。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.804%~0.830%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.69%，利率較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 及 5 年期指標券 A15108 均未成
交，週四等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.95%、1.84%。 
台股排擠效應、國際債市走勢偏空，加上油價續處高檔，通膨潛在威脅仍存，
12 月升息可能性未能完全排除，市場氣氛續偏觀望，預期台債殖利率將維持
高檔區間盤整 ， 預估5年券區間為 1.84%~1.90%，10年券區間為 1.95%~2.0%。

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 看好記憶體需求緊俏有利報價走升，維持「買進」 投資評等，目標價 800 元(6 x 27(F)PER)，
潛在上漲空間 51.8%。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/10/12 收盤價： 527.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 1336.5 億元新台幣， QoQ+61.9%，YoY+ 
611.7%，毛利率 85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利
益為 1068.9億元 ， 業外收入24.38億 ， 稅後淨利為 876.3
億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營收成
長及毛利雙雙成長受惠 平均銷售單價季增超過 六十位數
百分比，而季減中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分
比。 
➢ 展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，
且南亞科 AI相關產品已佔營收逾 20%，雖受限產能滿載
出貨量將無法再提升，然而預期合約價將持續於 26Q4上
漲 15~20%，營收及毛利率將持續創高。因此兆豐預估營
收 1556.04 億元，QoQ+16.4%，YoY+417.1%，毛利率 
88.1%，EPS為 30.35 元。 
➢ 展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於
DRAM產能持續滿載，預期 DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產
品將持續供不應求。1C 產品預期於 27Q1 開始驗證，有
利打入 AI伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此
兆豐預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，
毛利率 83.3/86.3%，EPS 為 77.85/133.2 元。南亞科目
前位於(3.9 X 27(F) PER)，看好記憶體需求緊俏報價持續
走升， 維持「買進」投資評等，目標價  800 元(6 x 
27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 51.8%。

營 運 分 析

南亞科技為全球前四大 DRAM廠商 
南亞科技主要致力 DRAM (動態隨機存取記憶體)的研發、設計、製造與銷售，
開發自主技術，發展智慧財產權，培育關鍵技術研發團隊，落實綠色科技及
智慧製造全面提升製程效能與產能，是全球第4大的 DRAM公司。公司已成
功開發 10 奈米級 DRAM 製程技術，將可微縮至少 3 個世代，並用以發展
DDR5及 LPDDR5 等下世代產品，強化公司自主技術開發與製程轉換佈局。
第二世代 10 奈米級製程技術(1B)產品 16Gb DDR5 已完成設計與試產，於
2024 年量產。南亞科技具備完整 DRAM 產品線，可因應智慧世代的發展，
滿足多元化的產品需求。預期 1C產品於 27Q1 開始驗證。

26Q3 ASP成長帶動營收毛利雙雙上升 
26Q3 營收  1336.5 億元新台幣， QoQ+61.9%，YoY+ 611.7%，毛利率
85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利益為 1068.9 億元，業外收入 24.38
億，稅後淨利為 876.3 億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營
收成長及毛利雙雙成長受惠平均銷售單價季增超過六十位數百分比，而季減
中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分比。

26Q4合約價持續上漲  
展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，且南亞科 AI 相關產
品已佔營收逾 20%，雖受限產能滿載出貨量將無法再提升，然而預期合約價
將持續於 26Q4 上漲 15~20%，營收及毛利率將持續創高。因此兆豐預估營
收 1556.04 億元，QoQ+16.4%，YoY+417.1%，毛利率 88.1%，EPS 為 30.35
元。

圖三、DRAM合約價報價走勢圖

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

南亞科完成 787.2 億元私募案，攜手四大客戶搶佔 AI 供應鏈先機 
南亞科日前完成總額新台幣  787.2 億元的私募認購，吸引四大核心客戶參
與。增資後，相關投資方持股比例達 10.19%。雙方同時簽署供應協議，確
保未來能穩定供應多元 DRAM 產品組合。主要目的在於強化南亞科與客戶
的戰略合作，透過 e-SSD 與網通產品等領域，建立 AI 及雲端服務供應商
（CSP）供應鏈中的雙贏局面。私募資金將注資新廠以提升產能，進一步確
立南亞科在 AI 價值鏈中的市場地位。本次私募後，南亞科總發行股數將達 
34.5 億股。

記憶體供不應求，產能持續擴充 
目前產能為 6.5 萬片/月，產能利用率維持滿載，2026Q1~Q3 資本支出 276
億，2026 以不超過 697 億元為上限，設備佔比五成．5A新廠第一階段 35,900
片/月 於 2026~2029 資本支出約 3,466 億．新廠預計於 2027 年初開始裝機，
主要生產 1B/1C/1D製程，預期 27年底開出 20k/月，2028 年達每月投片量
達 30k/月，主要因應網通、eSSD等產品之強勁需求。

AI 帶動記憶體強勁需求，有利報價持續走升 
展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於 DRAM 產能持續滿
載，預期DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產品將持續供不應求。1C產品預期於 27Q1
開始驗證，有利打入 AI 伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此兆豐
預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，毛利率83.3/86.3%，EPS
為 77.85/133.2 元。

記憶體持續供不應
求，報價持續走升。 
2027 新產能主要 用
於生產 1B/1C/1D 製
程。 
大型私募案確保未來
穩定供應多元 DRAM
產品組合。

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on preliminary JMTBA orders, which we
believe could be a catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
According to the Japan Machine Tool Builders' Association (JMTBA), headline
orders grew 13% MoM and 60% YoY in September 2026 (vs. +2% MoM/+65%
YoY in August 2026), to ¥223bn.

• Domestic orders were +24% MoM/+46% YoY (vs. -3% MoM/+62% YoY in
August), at ¥64bn.
• Overseas orders were +9% MoM/+67% YoY (vs. +5% MoM/+66% YoY in
August), at ¥159bn.
Our view:
• We think the sequential growth in overseas orders was consistent with
seasonality, while the strength on a YoY basis continued to point to an
upcycle scenario.
• Within overseas orders, we think China and Europe may have outperformed,
as manufacturing PMIs held up better (50.1/52.9 for China/Europe in

September vs. 49.8/52.7 in August). N.A is likely to be softer compared to
other regions, as its manufacturing PMI edged down slightly to 54.5 in
September (vs. 54.6 in August).
• We remain OW on both AirTAC (1590.TW) and Hiwin (2049.TW) as we see
company-specific drivers on top of the industry recovery trend:
° Airtac should continue to benefit from its own market share gains, and
valuation appears undemanding at 18x 2027e P/E (vs. 25x average since

2020).
° Hiwin should  see margin expansion in the coming quarters, driven by
higher utilization and price hikes, which we believe could be share price
catalysts. Current valuation of 29x 2027 P/E implies upside vs. peak
cycle valuation at 35-40x P/E. Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 12, 2026 06:26 PM GMT

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 105: 261014_daiwa_ ITEQ

Source PDF: all report pdf/261014_daiwa_ ITEQ.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We preview ITEQ’s 3Q26 results (due on 6 November). In our 
previous note, “Upgrading: strong beat in 2Q26 with improving outlook” (7 
August), we had underestimated the magnitude and durability of price hikes 
for E-glass and M4-and-below CCL. We raise our 2026-28E earnings by 
43-70% to reflect better-than-expected pricing and gross margin 
performance, which leads to our higher TP of TWD1,110 (from TWD600).

What’s the impact: Ongoing price hike of E-glass and M4-and-below 
CCL. This week, a major Japanese copper-clad laminate (CCL) vendor 
raised its entry-level CCL price by 200% to catch up with other global 
peers’ price hike level. One of the Chinese peers is also having a constant 
c.10% ASP hike on a monthly basis in 2026. We are increasingly confident 
in the management’s target for its blended price hike in 2H26. We now 
forecast c.30%/20% price hikes in 3Q26E and 4Q26E, respectively (vs. our 
previous estimates of 20%/10%). Looking ahead, we expect another 10% 
price hike per quarter in 1H27 (c.20% price hike in total), while pricing 
should gradually stabilise in 2H27 given the rising supply of E-glass, in our 
view. While the visibility towards 2H27 and 2028 remains relatively limited, 
we believe the key upside risks to our current forecast will be the stronger-
than-expected price hike magnitude and durability of the current up-cycle.

3Q26 results preview. After factoring in stronger-than-expected price 
hikes, ITEQ’s 3Q26 revenue grew c.35% QoQ, mainly driven by the 30% 
price hike despite utilisation rate staying at c.75%. We lift our gross margin 
forecast to c.30.3% (from 24.7% in our August note), in line with the August 
preliminary results’ pre-tax margin of 25.8%. With price hikes continuing, 
we expect the 4Q26E gross margin to expand to c.35%. Given disciplined 
capacity expansion through 2028, we expect the higher GM to be sustained 
through 2028. Beyond price hike, we believe the key longer-term growth 
drivers include: a) rising regular server demand and new platform adoption 
(using M7 grade CCL) in 2027; and b) M9 certification by more clients.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 43-70% to factor in 
the price hikes. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating, and raise our 12M TP to 
TWD1,110 (from TWD600), based on an unchanged target PER of 22x 
(higher than the range during the past 12 months of 5-18x) applied to our 1-
year-forward EPS. While we expect the current upcycle will eventually ease 
starting from mid-2027, we remain positive on the near-term price outlook. 
Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected regular server demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 36-60% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, mainly due to our more aggressive view on ITEQ’s price hikes 
and margin profile.

14 October 2026

3Q26 results preview: better-than-expected price hikes

➢ Expecting better price hikes from 2H26 to 1H27 
➢ Spec upgrades driven by new CPU platform adoption in 2027 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating; raising our 12M TP to TWD1,110

Company profile 
ITEQ Corporation is a leading manufacturer of high performance copper clad laminate materials used 
for the fabrication of printed circuit boards and was founded in April of 1997. ITEQ’s products find use in 
many different application areas, such as computational and communications applications, Radio 
Frequency and Microwave devices, Automotive applications, and High Density Interconnect solutions.

G
Executive/board
quality 2
All board members implement the company’s operation faithfully and fulfil their obligations with
discipline and care. Independent directors obey related laws and company regulations to protect
the rights of the company and stockholders.
Capital management 2
ITEQ paid a cash DPS of TWD3 in 2025, representing a 72% payout ratio. We believe ITEQ will
sustain a similar payout level of c.75% over 2026-28E. We see its balance between investment
and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 2 ITEQ’s sales to related parties were insignificant versus its total revenue.
S Supply chainmanagement 2 ITEQ requires all major raw material suppliers to comply with ESG, ethics, and regulatorystandards. Annual RBA audits were conducted for vendors, all rated A–C.
S Data security 1 ITEQ has established an ISO/IEC 27001-certified Information Security Management System,conducts quarterly training, and implements system recovery drills to ensure risk preparedness.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
TSMC was founded in 1987 and is listed in Taiwan 
(2330TT) and the US (TSM/NYS).TSMC is the largest and 
global leader in integrated circuit (IC) manufacturing. As a 
build-to-order foundry, it provides a wide range of value-
add activities: IC manufacturing, mask-making, IC design 
services, turnkey solutions, and process development. We 
attribute its success to its proven, winning business model, 
unparalleled scale advantage, optimized execution, and 
technology scope and depth. 5 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
The ongoing semi content growth in mobile, rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI), and proliferation of Internet of Things (IoT) 
should result in sustainable upside in aggregate computing 
power globally. TSMC, as the leading contract manufacturer 
of semiconductor chips, is in a good position to capitalize. 
We are structurally positive on TSMC and expect it to 
sustain over 15% growth with rising FCF from 2026 
onwards.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMWF / TSM) 
Our PO for TSMC is NT$3,260 per share (US$620 per ADR), based on 20x 2027E P/E. 
TSMC's valuation has traded in the range of 10-26x PE from 2017. 20x is in the mid to 
high end of the historical range, above the average P/E of 16x. We think a PE above the 
average is justified given TSMC's enhanced industry position and mid-to-high 50% 5-
year CAGR in AI should underpin a rerating

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) greater-than-expected slowdown in global 
smartphone/consumer electronics demand, (2) Intel's potential insourcing strategy and 
ambitions in foundry service, (3) execution risks on advanced nodes, and (4) higher than 
expected tariffs.

APR - Semiconductor Coverage Cluster

OSAT 展望樂觀，SOIC 機台具技術優勢 
 2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT:2026 年弘塑產能擴充一倍 ，1Q26 已
經完成擴建 ，3Q26 新產能已經滿載。 外包部分 ， 目前外包比例33%，
未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外包產能受
限，同業也在搶外包產能。2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT，今年晶
圓代工客戶營收高於 OSAT，2027 年 OSAT 營收超過晶圓代工客戶，
SOIC 機台去年小量，今年量增，2027 跟 2026 差不多略增，2028
才會明顯大增，主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度。 
 2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量會很明顯放大:PLP 及 CoPoS 設備機
台單價很高，初估有機會是 CoWoS 翻倍，PLP 進度比較快，PLP
規格弘塑 310*310、510*515、600*600 都有，再大到 700 以上就不
做了，晶圓代工客戶規格是 310*310 但目前進度比較慢，還在實驗
線階段。力成 510*515 進度最快，4Q26 會拉第一批機台，2027 隨
力成購置的友達廠進度持續拉機台，2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量
會很明顯放大。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 3000 元：基本面無變化，預估數字也沒做調
整，故維持買進評等 ，預估 2026 年 EPS 64.81 元，2027 年 EPS 93.97
元，目標價 3000 元。(2027 PER 30x)

弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商 
 弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商 
弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商，主要利基產品為半導體後段濕製程設備，並延
伸相關化學品、檢測設備代理及軟體解決方案。1H26 產品結構可區分為機台檢
測產品(65%)、化學產品(20%)、代理設備(15%)。2013 年合併化學品公司添鴻
(CLC)、2018 年底合併通路商佳霖(ClC)、併購資訊系統廠太引(Tyne)，投資美
國 X-Ray 檢測公司 SVXR。 客戶為國內半導體 、 光電 、記憶體及面板產業大廠，
包括台積電、矽品、日月光、力成、星科金朋、精材、聯電、茂矽、采鈺、穏
懋、中芯、宏達等。

2Q26 毛 利率 39.37%， 低於預
期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
弘塑 2Q26 EPS 11.18 元，低於元富預估的 12.34 元，獲利達成率 91%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 17.27 億元，+8%QOQ，+6%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，
較 1Q26 回升 5.1 個百分點，然仍低於預估的 41%，主要是外包成本及急單增
加影響部分毛利率，預期 3Q26 毛利率持續向上改善，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元。

弘塑 3Q26 營收 22.67 億元，+31.3%QOQ，+51.8%YOY，營收達成率 98%，
略低於預期，預估 3Q26 毛利率 41%，預估 3Q26 EPS 16.47 元。

營運逐季成長，OSA T展望樂觀

營運逐季成長，OSA T展望樂觀 
2026 年弘塑擴產一倍 ，1Q26 已經完成擴建 ，3Q26 新產能已經滿載。 外包部分 ，
目前外包比例 33%，未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外
包產能受限 ， 同業也在搶外包產能 。2027 年擴產再 50-100%， 以租用廠房為主。
2027 主要成長動能還是 OSAT，今年晶圓代工客戶營收高於 OSAT，2027 年
OSAT 營收超過晶圓代工客戶，SOIC 機台去年小量，今年量增，2027 跟 2026
差不多略增 ，2028 才會明顯大增 ， 主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度，
SOIC 機台種類 6-7 種。

續拉機台，2028-2029 年 PLP 量會很明顯放大。

公司簡介 
樺漢為全球工業電腦大廠 ， 為鴻海轉投資工
業電腦廠，產品包括 POS(point of sale)系

2026 年 1-9 月營收占比：工業物聯網

主要競爭對手：

張智皓 chihhao.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

樺漢 (6414 TT) 
在手訂單維持 2550 億元

永豐觀點 
三大事業體持續成長，第三季營收同期新高，未來營運將聚焦於 Physical AI 市場
發展。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進投資建議：主係考量(1) 實體 AI 應用落地，包含 AMR、自動駕駛、智慧
工廠、機器人等應用，樺漢從產品、平台、軟硬整合延伸，潛在市場成長空間相當
大；(2) Kontron 完成整併，策略整合綜效逐漸顯現，擴大競爭優勢；(3) 除三大
事業體接單動能穩健外， 智能零售市場成長性高，樺漢將從硬體製造跨足到軟體平
台 ， 目前合作對象皆外國內外知名餐飲及零售商等 。 評價方面 ， 以2027 年稅後 EPS 
28.5 元，目前本益比約 14 倍，過往歷史本益比區間為 13~22 倍，考量在手訂單
持續墊高，營運規模效益可期，故維持買進的投資建議，目標價485 元(17X 2027 
EPS)。

ESG 評析 
樺漢企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 A 等。

樺漢為全球工業電腦大廠：樺漢科技股份有限公司成立於 1999 年，為鴻海轉投資
工業電腦廠，產品包括 POS(point of sale)系統、ATM 系統、工業控制系統、車載
系統 、 人機互動式產品和其他周邊如處理器 、 記憶體等產品 。 最大股東為鴻海集團 ，
持有 24.1%股權(包括寶鑫國際 22.7%、鴻揚創投 1.4%)。樺漢主要分成三大事業
群：工業物聯網、智慧軟體與方案、智慧工廠與廠務。2026 年 1-9 月營收占比：
工業物聯網 45.7%、智慧工廠與廠務 42.6％、智慧軟體與方案 11.7%。 
邊緣運算及半導體應用， 3Q26 營收單季新高 ：樺漢 3Q26 營收 496.4 億元
(+2.6%QoQ，+47.4%YoY)， 成長主係來自於工業物聯網中邊緣運算需求強勁以及
智慧工廠受惠於半導體客戶廠務工程進度持續推薦 ， 智慧軟體與方案部門因整併及
專 案 進 度 影 響 略 微 衰 退， 研 究 部 預 估 在 產 品 組 合 差 異 不 大 下 ， 毛 利 率
19.5%(+0.2pts QoQ，-2.2ptsYoY)，營利率 5.9%(+0.3ptsQoQ，+2.1ptsYoY)，
稅後淨利 10.35 億元(-1.5%QoQ，+8.9%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 6.59 元。 
訂單能見度佳，2026 年高成長不變：展望第四季，樺漢維持全年高成長目標，三
大事業體出貨狀況良好，其中 NCRV 全年預估貢獻 160 億元，Kontron 亦完成收
購並持續深化營運綜效，智慧工廠業務訂單將逐季認列營收。研究部預估樺漢
4Q26 營收 519.9 億元(+4.7%QoQ，+31.6%YoY)，毛利率 19.6%(+0.1ptsQoQ，
-1ptsYoY)，營利率 6.7%(+0.8ptsQoQ，-1.2ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 11.37 億元
(+9.8%QoQ，+30.6%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 7.24 元。預估 2026 年營收 1871.3 億
元 (+31.5%YoY)， 受 產 品 組 合 影 響 ， 毛 利 率19.6%(-0.9pts YoY) ， 營 利 率
6%(+1.4pts YoY)，稅後淨利 39.75 億元(+23.7%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 25.32 元。 
在手訂單 2550 億元，朝向成為高毛利軟硬整合系統商：樺漢集團策略朝向聚焦高
毛利高成長事業，如從 Physical AI 相關產業進行全面整合，以成為系統整合商為
營運重點，目前在手訂單維持 2550 億高檔並持續推升。此外，持續整合Kontron
營運創造縱效，透過技術整合及市場聚焦，管理層目標 Kontron2030 年為營收達
26 億歐元、EBITDA 目標 4.2 億歐元，並透過持續降低供應鏈採購成本及製造成
本，擴大營運槓桿優勢，提高軟體等高價值產品。獲利方面，預估高毛利產品營收
占比將持續提升，產業聚焦於零售餐飲、智慧能源與樓宇、交通、醫療長照、智慧
城市與公共安全、智慧製造等。展望未來，預估 2027 年 NCRV 營收年增 15%，
智慧零售將成為下一個成長動能 ，樺漢將打造平台的生台戲 ， 從方案代理商製產品
銷售並與 Nvidia 及聯發科技術合作。長期來看，無論是透過策略合作或者併購，

Document 231: AI芯片需求持续释放，寒武纪Q3营收同比增长104.47%，净利润增147.74%｜财报见闻

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI芯片需求持续释放，寒武纪Q3营收同比增长104.47%，净利润增147.74%｜财报见闻.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23

### Page 1

寒武纪 Q3 营收 35.31 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 104.47% ；归⺟净利润 14.04 亿元，同⽐增⻓
147.74% 。前三季度营收、净利润分别增⻓ 106.76% 和 131.49% ，公司称增⻓主要得益于
持续拓展市场、推动⼈⼯智能应⽤落地。与此同时，存货增⾄ 116.65 亿元，预付款项和应
付票据也⼤幅增加。
摘要：
寒武纪三季度营收、净利润继续保持⾼速增⻓，⼈⼯智能应⽤落地带动业务规模进⼀步扩⼤。
10 ⽉ 23 ⽇，寒武纪披露 2026 年第三季度报告，第三季度实现营业收⼊ 35.31 亿元，同⽐增⻓
104.47% ；归属于上市公司股东的净利润为 14.04 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 147.74% 。
前三季度，公司累计实现营业收⼊ 95.26 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 106.76% ；归⺟净利润 37.15 亿元，同
⽐增⻓ 131.49% 。营收和利润增速均超过 100% ，净利润增速⾼于营收增速，盈利规模进⼀步扩
⼤。
寒武纪在财报中表⽰，营收增⻓主要得益于持续拓展市场、积极助⼒⼈⼯智能应⽤落地。随着

AI 算⼒需求增⻓及相关应⽤逐步推进，公司业务规模持续扩张。
与此同时，公司资产负债表也出现明显变化。截⾄三季度末，总资产达到 223.64 亿元，较年初
增⻓ 66.43% ；存货、预付款项和应付票据均⼤幅增加，反映出业务扩张伴随采购备货及供应链
结算规模上升。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

营收与利润保持⾼增⻓，主营业务盈利能⼒提升
2026 年前三季度，寒武纪实现营业收⼊ 95.26 亿元，较上年同期的 46.07 亿元增⻓ 106.76% 。公
司将营收增⻓归因于持续拓展市场、积极助⼒⼈⼯智能应⽤落地。
盈利⽅⾯，前三季度归⺟净利润为 37.15 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 131.49% ；扣除⾮经常性损益后的净利
润为 35.23 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 148.30% ，增速⾼于归⺟净利润。第三季度，归⺟净利润和扣⾮净利
润分别同⽐增⻓ 147.74% 和 168.13% 。
前三季度，公司⾮经常性损益合计约 1.91 亿元，其中政府补助约 1.23 亿元，⾦融资产公允价值变
动及处置收益约 5552 万元。扣⾮净利润增速⾼于归⺟净利润，表明扣除⾮经常性损益后的利润
增⻓更快。
此外，前三季度扣除股份⽀付影响后的净利润为 39.05 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 120.32% 。这⼀指标反映
了股份⽀付费⽤对当期利润的影响，与会计⼝径下的归⺟净利润有所不同。
研发投⼊持续增加，经营现⾦流仍有波动

研发⽅⾯，寒武纪第三季度研发投⼊为 5.23 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 73.66% ；前三季度累计投⼊ 12.25 亿
元，同⽐增⻓ 45.35% 。由于营收增速⾼于研发投⼊增速，前三季度研发投⼊占营收的⽐例降⾄
12.86% ，同⽐下降 5.43 个百分点。研发投⼊总额仍在增⻓，公司继续加⼤技术研发⼒度。

现⾦流⽅⾯，前三季度经营活动产⽣的现⾦流量净额为 2.28 亿元，上年同期为净流出 2930 万
元，整体实现由负转正。不过，第三季度单季经营活动现⾦流净额为 -8368 万元，显⽰现⾦流在
季度间存在波动。
截⾄三季度末，公司预付款项为 30.44 亿元，较年初的 7.45 亿元⼤幅增加；存货为 116.65 亿元，
也显著⾼于年初的约 49.43 亿元。预付款项和存货同步增⻓，可能与采购备货及供应链安排有
关。
存货与应付票据⼤幅增加，供应链资⾦规模上升
截⾄三季度末，寒武纪总资产为 223.64 亿元，较年初增⻓ 66.43% ；归属于上市公司股东的净资
产为 151.24 亿元。资产规模扩⼤之际，公司营运资⾦相关科⽬也出现明显变化。

资产端，存货从年初的约 49.43 亿元增⾄ 116.65 亿元，预付款项从 7.45 亿元增⾄ 30.44 亿元。负
债端，应付票据从 3.51 亿元增⾄ 49.66 亿元，应付账款从 7.65 亿元增⾄ 18.27 亿元，合同负债则
从约 61 万元增⾄ 1.41 亿元。
上述变化表明，公司采购备货及供应链结算规模明显扩⼤。存货增加有助于为后续⽣产和交付
提供资源保障，但也意味着更多资⾦沉淀于库存。未来存货能否顺利转化为收⼊和现⾦流，将
成为观察公司经营质量的重要指标。
与此同时，公司资产负债率从年初的 11.87% 升⾄ 32.36% 。尽管归属于上市公司股东的净资产仍
达 151.24 亿元，但随着存货、预付款项及应付票据增加，后续仍需关注库存消化、供应商结算
及经营现⾦流的变化。

Document 243: Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 mi

Source PDF: all report pdf/Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01

### Page 1

13028181     1

Economy 
Business Checkpoint: Looking into the logistics 
15 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Small and mid-sized businesses entered Q3 in relatively solid financial condition, with profitability growth up across both 
groups, according to Bank of America business account data. Nearly half of estimated tariff payments had been refunded by 
September, though the share of these refunds relative to revenue has peaked. 
• In Bank of America business payments data, logistics spending growth per client was up four times as much for small than mid-
sized firms. Additionally, distribution and inventory transactions growth per client has outpaced spending since the spring of 
2025. 
• Growing transportation bottlenecks are re-emerging ahead of the holiday season. Trucking capacity has tightened as driver 
availability remains constrained, according to BofA Global Research. This has pushed freight rates higher and driven strong 
transportation sector payroll growth, particularly among mid-sized businesses, according to Bank of America payments data.

Business Checkpoint is a new, regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It provides a real-time view of the financial health, opportunities 
and challenges facing small and mid-sized businesses across the US economy, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America's proprietary 
data. By analyzing activity among both small and mid-sized firms, Business Checkpoint offers timely insights into business profitability, hiring, 
investment and spending trends. Such data is not intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, 
financial condition or performance of Bank of America. 
Businesses end Q3 in fair shape 
While profitability growth remained positive for both small and mid-sized firms in the third quarter, small firms outpaced their 
mid-sized counterparts for the second consecutive quarter (Exhibit 2). Still, there’s been a notable reversal in the profitability of 
mid-sized businesses, growing this quarter after declining slightly for the past two.  
The continued rollout of tariff refunds is serving as a tailwind to some firms' cash positions. That is particularly true for small 
firms. According to Bank of America account data, by September approximately 47% of estimated International Emergency 
Economic Powers Act-related tariff payments had been refunded to companies with less than $50 million in annual revenue.  
Exhibit 1: Tariff refunds as a share of income for businesses <$50M 
in annual revenue peaked in June

### Page 2

2 15 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

According to Bank of America business payments data, the percentage point difference in tariff refunds as a share of revenue 
for firms with less than $50 million in annual revenue spiked in June but dropped to nearly zero in September. At the same time, 
the percentage point difference in tariff payments as a share of expenses began rising again relative to the 2023 average 
(Exhibit 1). This suggests that while tariffs continue to affect companies, the refund program has provided temporary relief for 
some businesses.  
Wholesale trade along with transportation and warehousing posted solid profitability growth across business size 
However, the benefits appear uneven. Such refunds have boosted cash flow, but they have not fully insulated firms from broader 
cost pressures.  
Looking across industries, mid-sized construction and wholesale firms had the strongest profitability growth in September 
(Exhibit 3). Notably, transportation and warehousing, as well as wholesale trade were among the few sectors showing positive 
profitability growth for both small and mid-sized firms last month. 
The persistence of logistics, freight and inventory-related cost pressures may help explain why profitability gains have remained 
relatively concentrated in transportation and wholesale trade. However, these sectors also have a higher exposure to rising gas 
prices within their margins (read more on this in the August Small Business Checkpoint). 
Exhibit 3: For both small and mid-sized businesses, profitability growth was positive in transportation and warehousing as well as wholesale trade 
in September 
Profitability growth by sector and business size in September (YoY%, six-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The cost of maintaining supply chains is rising 
In fact, the prices paid component of the September services reading from the Institute for Supply Management indicates still-
building price pressure for firms (Exhibit 4). This marked the highest reading since July 2022.   
Using Bank of America internal data, we identified payments to logistics and freight companies to assess a key part of supply 
chain costs. In June, logistics and freight payments’ growth per client from small businesses grew nearly 30%, though has 
moderated to 12.7% YoY in September (Exhibit 5). Meanwhile, mid-sized firms’ payments growth in this category was up just 
3.2% YoY in September. 
The divergence suggests logistics costs may be disproportionately affecting smaller firms, who are typically less capable of 
absorbing rising costs than larger firms. Larger companies also often benefit from dedicated logistics contracts and greater 
purchasing power, while smaller ones may be more exposed to spot-market transportation costs and fluctuations in freight 
pricing.

Evidence of more moving parts in companies’ supply chains 
Yet there is increasing evidence that tariffs and geopolitics are adding complexity to small and mid-sized companies’ supply 
chains. 
In Bank of America payments data, average distribution and inventory transaction growth per business client has increased 
steadily since the spring of 2025 despite spending growth in this category falling in September (Exhibit 6). Rising transaction 
growth alongside softer spending growth suggests businesses may be engaging with more suppliers, shipments or inventory 
movements even as overall procurement outlays moderates.  
Furthermore, in May 2026, the Global Supply Chain Pressure Index reached its highest reading since the summer of 2022 and has 
continued to remain elevated into October (Exhibit 7). This further emphasizes that transportation bottlenecks, delays and 
procurement challenges are becoming more common globally. While current readings remain below the extreme levels observed 
during the pandemic, the recent move higher indicates supply chain normalization may be reversing. 
Exhibit 6: Average distribution and inventory transaction growth 
per business client has increased steadily since the spring of 2025

The trials of trucking  
As of October 1, BofA Global Research's proprietary bi-weekly Truckload Demand Indicator (see Methodology) remained below 
its historical growth signal (Exhibit 9) and the Truck Capacity Indicator, which reflects shippers' views of available capacity, 
tightened significantly, falling from 39.2 to 28.4 (Exhibit 8). This suggests shippers are seeing fewer trucks available relative to 
demand than in the previous survey. 
According to BofA Global Research, the current trucking upcycle is being driven by constrained supply rather than a significant 
improvement in freight demand. While any positive movement in demand is an important indicator as the industry moves into 
peak shipping season between late September and early December, tighter capacity appears to be the dominant force 
supporting freight rates. 
Exhibit 8: Shippers’ view of available capacity has fallen 
significantly as of October 1, 2026 
BofA Truckload Capacity Indicator by year (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 9: The Demand Indicator has been sub-60 for the past two 
months after 4.5 months above 
BofA Truckload Demand Indicator (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The reduction in capacity reflects several factors, including stricter commercial driver's license (CDL) and training requirements, 
increased compliance and enforcement measures, and other regulatory changes that have reduced the pool of qualified drivers. 
This has also likely driven some wage inflation within the industry. According to Bank of America payments data, transportation 
and warehousing payroll growth per client has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses (Exhibit 10).  
Having to pay higher wages suggests labor shortages remain a challenge in the sector. Firms may increasingly need to offer 
higher compensation to attract and retain workers as trucking capacity tightens. In turn, such capacity puts upward pressure on 
freight rates, particularly in the spot market where small businesses may be more impacted. 
Exhibit 10: Transportation and warehousing payroll growth has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses 
Payroll payments per client by industry and business size (monthly, YoY%, three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Logistics payments tend to increase in the second half of the year 
September marks a key run-up to the holiday season (read more on signs of holiday spending in the October Consumer 
Checkpoint), with spending on ocean shipping, containers, and other transportation services steadily accelerating ahead of the 
peak shopping period.  
In fact, over the past two years, the month of November has seen ocean shipping/container spending volumes well above their 
2023-2025 monthly average (Exhibit 11). The seasonal pattern likely reflects inventory restocking and holiday-related shipping 
activity. Ocean shipping volumes typically accelerate during the fall months as businesses prepare for year-end demand. 
Exhibit 11: Ocean shipping/container spending volumes were well above their 2023-2025 monthly average in November 
Logistics and freight average payments by category (monthly, indexed, 100 = monthly 2023-2025 average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Distributors and inventory payments’ growth fell ahead of holiday season 
Changes in logistics and freight spending often provide an early signal of inventory trends, as distributors account for a large 
share of supply chain-related spending (Exhibit 12). 
The sharp moderation in distribution and inventory spending growth per client for both small and mid-sized businesses indicates 
that much of the large-scale inventory accumulation seen earlier in 2025 has already occurred (Exhibit 13). In other words, 
businesses appear to be replenishing inventories for seasonal demand rather than significantly expanding stockpiles as they did 
earlier in the year. 
Overall, businesses seem to be entering the holiday season in relatively stable financial condition, supported by tariff refunds 
and positive profitability growth. However, rising logistics costs, tighter trucking capacity and elevated supply chain pressures 
suggest operating conditions are becoming more challenging.

chain-related payments

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business and/or Business Banking clients with a deposit account or a 
Small Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of 
America per Business client data represents activity spending from active Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and are 
generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue. Mid-sized businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America 
and are generally defined as $5mm-$50mm in annual sales revenue. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Revenue tiers are determined by the combination of following factors: 1) stated revenue on small businesses credit applications, 2) actual 
account inflow into Bank of America Deposit Accounts, and 3) third party revenue estimation.  
The alternative hiring indicator consists of payments from Bank of America business clients to business-focused hiring firms which include both 
direct deposits through Automated Clearing House (ACH) and payments via credit and debit cards. 
Supply chain-related spending is comprised of payments to logistics, freight, distribution, inventory, delivery service, and trucking companies. 
These are identified from merchant names and MCC codes. 
BofA Global Research’s trucking indicators are a bi-weekly diffusion-style survey of shipper demand expectations over the next 0–3 months, 
built from responses from surveyed shippers. 
Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 263: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations

Standard Chartered Bank

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist

Document 284: El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

30 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.
helene.drouot@bnpparibas.com

tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.0%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.3%. 
 Of the 121 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 82.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 12.5%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 11.7%. 
 63.6% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 18.9. 
 During the week of Oct. 26, 176 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $775.4B 
from $760.7B). Four of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-7.5%) and materials (-6.9%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (27.7%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 1.9% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-5.4B) and health care (-4.3B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-
level decreases in earnings, while the energy (13.1B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 14.7B since the start of the quarter.

121 companies in the S&P 500 Index have reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these companies, 82.6% reported 
earnings above analyst expectations and 14.9% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 6.6% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth rate 
declines to 26.3%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $775.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.8B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Health Care sector 
has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (135.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $60.5B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.7B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (533.8%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (131.9%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 34%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (66.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $257.1B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154.1B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (135.7%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (69.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 25.8%. 
The Health Care sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (1.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $72.6B in 
26Q3, compared to earnings of $71.5B in 25Q3. 3 of the 10 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Pharmaceuticals (-12.6%) and Health Care Facilities (-5.4%) sub-industries have the 
lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 11.6%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q4 is 30.1%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 25.5%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $813.7B in 26Q4, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $625.5B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q4. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q4. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Real Estate sector 
has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q4. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (142.8%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $59B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $24.3B in 25Q4. 3 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (355.7%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (171.9%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 51.5%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (56%) of any sector. It is expected to 
earn $291.5B in 26Q4, compared to earnings of $186.8B in 25Q4. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (119.9%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (70.1%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 18.5%. 
The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (4.1%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $15.2B in 
26Q4, compared to earnings of $14.6B in 25Q4. 2 of the 13 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Telecom Tower Reits (-12.4%) and Multi-Family Residential Reits (-0.1%) sub-industries 
have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 6.8%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 302 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 39 positive

surprises and 30 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

Subject: SpaceXSI might reverse the negative sentiment on AI infrastructure investment

SpaceXSI might reverse the negative sentiment on AI infrastructure investment

SpaceXSI might reverse the
negative sentiment on AI
infrastructure investment
SpaceXSI 三⼤業務超預期，全線業務扭虧轉盈，有機會帶動之前弱
勢的 AI 族群短期復甦
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
OCT 20 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP

Executive summary
SpaceXSI 公布 3Q26 財報，三⼤業務  Starlink, ⽕箭， AI 營收，獲利⼤超預
期，全線業務扭虧轉盈，有機會帶動之前持續弱勢的 AI 基礎建設及產業鏈板塊
短期復甦，儘管如此，公司加碼資本開⽀，⾃由現⾦持續流出，估值偏⾼仍是
值得投資⼈關注的⾵險，但  capital density 及  capital return 的季度變化相
當正⾯，這季度 AI 營收趕超  CoreWeave, 明年底趕超甲⾻⽂， SpaceXSI 應
該有機會成為  NeoCloud 新⿓頭。
Sales beat across all segments
SpaceXSI reports 3Q26 sales of US$14.575bn, up 87% qoq and
161% yoy, which was 13% above Bloomberg consensus estimates
of US$12.875bn. 3Q26 sales beat was across all segments with a

24% sales beat on Space, 8% beat on Starlink, and 20% beat on AI.
Operating proﬁts beat across all segments
SpaceXSI reports 3Q26 operating proﬁts of US$2.79bn at operating
margin of 19%, which was 131% higher than Bloomberg consensus
estimates of US$1.21bn at operating margin of 12% and turned
proﬁts from 2Q26’s -2% operating margin. We attributed the
improvement across all segments with 98% more losses cut on
Space vs. consensus, 27% beat on Starlink, and US$445mn higher

### Page 2

proﬁts on AI (-49% operating margin in 2Q26 to +6% in 3Q26),
resulting 3Q26 adj EBITDA improvement to US$8.8bn from 2Q26’s
US$3.54b.
Source: Bloomberg estimates
付費訂閱讀者可分享我們對  SpaceXSI 資本開⽀增
加，⾃由現⾦流出， capital density 及 capital
return 變化，趕超  CoreWeave ，甲⾻⽂的看法
Subscribed
Cutting capital density despite capex hike and negative free
cash ﬂow
Given SpaceXSI continues to boost its 3Q26 capex to US$19.593bn,
up 7% qoq and 185% yoy, coupled with short of cash ﬂow from

operations (US$9.63bn), the company reports a negative free cash
ﬂow of nearly US$10bn. However, due to a stronger than expected
sales improvement, capital density was cut to 134% in 3Q26 from
2Q26’s 235%.

### Page 3

Source: SpaceXSI, Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
Passing CoreWeave, catching Oracle on AI business
SpaceXSI reports US$7.56bn on AI business in 3Q26, up 195% qoq
and 830% yoy, growth accelerated from 2Q26’s 247% yoy. AI
business in 3Q26 accounted for 52% of SpaceXSI total sales, up
from 33% in 2Q26. As a result, we estimate SpaceXSI AI business to
exceed CoreWeave sales in 3Q26 and might exceed Oracle cloud
sales by 4Q27. Driven by a dramatic operating margin improvement

考，並⾮投資建議，個⼈應審慎評估⾵險，如因此造成任何損失，本⼈不負任

### Page 1

Oil rallied yesterday as markets grow increasingly concerned
about the risk of a renewed escalation in tensions between the
US and Iran

Warren Patterson and Ewa Manthey
Energy – European gas storage draws start
Oil prices surged yesterday, with front-month ICE Brent settling 4%
higher at just above $113/bbl. The rally is being driven less by
current fundamentals than by mounting supply concerns. Markets
are increasingly worried about renewed escalation in the Persian
Gulf, a scenario that could threaten oil ﬂows that have so far
remained remarkably resilient.
Reports suggest President Trump is considering fresh strikes on Iran,
while the US is reinforcing its military presence in the region with the
deployment of a second aircraft carrier. Meanwhile, there are
reports that US troops have been evacuated from some bases in
the Middle East. Clearly, these developments don’t paint an
optimistic picture. They call into question our long-held view that
the US midterm elections would bring de-escalation and

### Page 2

WTI TTF Treasuries Persian Gulf Natural gas
LNG Gold Geopolitics Brent
normalisation in ﬂows and oil prices. If anything, re-escalation
seems the most likely path. As a result, our expectation that ICE
Brent would average $80/bbl over 4Q26 is looking unattainable.
Even if tensions don't re-escalate, the mere possibility is likely to
keep a sizeable geopolitical risk premium embedded in prices.
European gas prices also remain extremely sensitive to

developments in the Persian Gulf, with LNG ﬂows increasingly likely
to be largely absent from the market for at least much of the
Northern Hemisphere winter. TTF rallied 3% yesterday, settling
above EUR90/MWh, its highest since December 2022. EU gas
storage also appears to have peaked at just under 75% full, with
storage falling marginally on Tuesday.
Metals — Gold falls as energy risks reignite
inﬂation concerns
Gold prices failed to beneﬁt from yesterday's equity sell-oﬀ and the

signiﬁcant fall in US Treasury yields, with the 10-year yield falling
back below 5%. Instead, gold prices came under pressure. Spot
prices fell 2.8% and are nearing $4,000/oz. The gold market
appears more focused on higher oil prices and what it could mean
for inﬂation — and ultimately central bank policy. With US inﬂation
still above the central bank’s 2% target, higher energy prices could
intensify price pressures and strengthen the case for tighter

monetary policy.
LME copper prices declined for a second consecutive session, falling
more than 1.9% yesterday, as rising exchange inventories and a
selloﬀ in AI-related stocks weighed on broader risk sentiment. LME
data showed copper inventories increased by 27,675 tonnes for a
fourth consecutive session to 272,950 tonnes as of yesterday, the
highest level since 27 July 2026.
